# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document is a companion to
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md). It
> expands the RFC’s phase sequence into **level-3 tasks that can be committed and verified
> independently**.
>
> The phase order and acceptance gates are governed solely by the body of RFC-039. This document is
> responsible only for decomposing the work into executable granularity and recording conflicts that
> remain undecided.

## Scope and Positioning

| Level       | Count                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Phases, corresponding one-to-one to the phase sequence in RFC-039                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **Level 2** | 72 (2026-10-13: +2 = 3.13 / 3.14; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.15; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.16; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17 / 3.18; 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4; 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19 / S7 / S8; 2026-10-18: +2 = 3.21 / S9 (the original registration number in this group was 3.20; migrated to 3.21 on 2026-10-21, and 3.20 was reassigned to “Existing false positives in dead-code analysis”; see the closure record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); 2026-10-21: +2 = 0.5 / 3.20; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10 (the original S9 number from the 4.4 batch was already used by “Pre-release audit abstraction closure,” so it was deferred); 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22; 2026-10-25: +1 = 3.23; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11)                                                                                                   | Task groups derived from the “Implementation Points” sections of each document + the 2026-10-07 audit addendum (P3’s 3.4) + the 2026-10-10/11 ruling supplements (P3’s 3.5/3.6/3.7/3.8/3.9) + the 2026-10-12 registration (P3’s 3.10) + the 2026-10-12 discovery supplement (P3’s 3.11) + the 2026-10-12 second discovery supplement (P3’s 3.12) |
| **Level 3** | 193 (2026-10-13: +2 = 3.13.1 / 3.14.1; 2026-10-14: +2 = 3.15.1 / 3.16.1; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17.1 / 3.18.1; 2026-10-16: +2 = 4.6.2 / 4.9.6 (`#450`, user ruling A); 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4.1; 2026-10-17: +1 = 4.9.7 (`#464`); 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19.1 / S7.1 / S8.1; 2026-10-18: +12 = 4.9.8 / 3.14.2 / 4.10.4 / 4.10.5 / 3.21.1–3.21.4 (formerly 3.20.1–3.20.4, migrated on 2026-10-21) / S9.1 / S9.2 / S2.2 / 4.4.4; 2026-10-21: +7 = 3.6.2 / 3.6.3 / 3.6.4 / 0.5.1 / 0.5.2 / 3.20.1 / 3.20.2; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10.1; 2026-10-22: +5 = 3.6.5 / 3.6.6 / 4.10.6 / 4.10.7 / 4.10.8; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.11.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 0.5.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22.1; 2026-10-25: +2 = 3.23.1 / 3.18.2; +2 = 3.20.3 / 3.18.3; 2026-10-26: +1 = 3.20.4; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12.1; +1 = 9.12.2; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11.1) | Independently committable actions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

**Reserved numbers (not reclaimed)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`. None of these
three has had a corresponding entry since the WBS was established—the entire history of this
document has zero matches for the `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1` headings
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can verify this), and there
are no references elsewhere in the repository. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of `05` (test
revival and completion): that phase was split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`, so P8’s numbering is
aligned with its phase numbers (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so on).

**Covered by this document**: task decomposition, dependency relationships, parallelization groups,
and binding to acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: the design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) or the rationale
for phase ordering (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: newly registered level-2/level-3 rows **must
contain two fields**—“**Owning phase**” (the P to which the task genuinely belongs, which may differ
from its section) and “**Blocks the mainline**.” `Awaiting scheduling` is no longer the default
state; an unmarked task is considered **not schedulable**. Numbers must be **unique across the
entire document** (tasks on separate tracks use their track number, such as `029g.x`, and must not
occupy `N.x`). **Checkoff/completion registration is based on “task id + evidence (gate metric, run
id, issue number, file:line),” not commit numbers**—`pull --rebase` rewrites local hashes, so
hard-coded numbers inevitably become invalid (2026-10-13 example: `94ad03ce4`→`631eb3e3c`). Use
`git log` to find commit numbers.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Decomposition

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” from eight documents into this table, **five hard
gaps in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been closed:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Resolution                                                                                                                                                        |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps**. RFC-039 defines P5 (checker decomposition) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but not one of the seven documents describes how to perform the decomposition; `01-routing.md` contains a single mention of `annotations.rs` in its directory tree, the only mention in the entire batch.              | P5’s steps are supplied by this document (see below).                                                                                                             |
| G2  | **The three `include!` conversions have no owner and no implementation**. RFC-039 lists them under P4; `02` assigns the referenced work to itself; `06` also lists them under S7. However, `02`’s change list (6 additions + 20 modifications) contains **no `semantic_tokens.rs`**.                                                               | The implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 in this document (after P4 is complete); the references in RFC-039 and `06` now point here.                      |
| G3  | **Three CI-script inventories coexist**. `RFC` lists four `scripts/ci/*.py`; `01` lists four scripts + one `cargo test`; `07` lists four different checks; `04` also requires `check-synth-boundary.py`. After deduplication, there are **at least nine new scripts and no unified inventory**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9. | See §P9 in this document for the unified inventory (11 scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents now reference that table.                           |
| G4  | **Conflict over the life or death of `precedence_inline.rs`**. `06` counts it among the “1,005 lines that must be revived,” while B6 in the same document says it “must be deleted together with the `Precedence` enum”; `05` advocates deletion.                                                                                                  | **Revive in P1; delete with Pratt in P8** (decision registered as C1)—first restore CI coverage, then perform the deletion in P8’s 8.6 alongside the dead ladder. |
| G5  | **Conflicting descriptions of the L2 goal**. RFC-039 and this directory’s index say “grammar-driven syntax,” while `05` self-evaluates that it “**did not use true grammar-driven parsing (LALRPOP), and only achieved declarative table lookup**.”                                                                                                | Converged on **a fully grammar-driven implementation using LALRPOP** (`05` §Syntax: Fully Grammar-Driven); the G7/G8 acceptance gates enforce that commitment.    |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0  仓库维护机制与代码放置规程     08        ← 新增，先立规矩
P1  复活孤儿测试并修缺陷           06 §S1
P2  建立等价性判据基线             07 全部
P3  修正确性漏洞（最小方案）        02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4  阶段契约与统一 Driver          02
P5  checker 文件内拆分             02 + 本文补齐
P6  类型表示单一化                 03
P7  中间表示 SSA 化                04      } 可并行
P8  前端范式变更                   05      }
P9  防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单）     01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正             06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: the three prohibitions (do not invent; do not pad indefinitely; do not
patch when a refactor is required) constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be established
before work starts; otherwise, while reviving tests in P1, people will already be adding `mod`
declarations to `tests/mod.rs`—precisely the cause of the G4 conflict.

The fix = reduce `stderr` to `diagnostics: [[code, file, line], …]` + retain only **crash marker lines** (panic / ICE / backtrace header), add `strip_ansi` (run-side color codes) and position lookahead (the position is still found when source code appears between the diagnostic header and `-->`); `exit` / `stdout` / `check.*` / `expectation` remain unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 0.5.1        | The 356-line baseline is regenerated and the `check-corpus-parity` diff is empty; **rerunning the probe after changing locale text still produces zero diff** (justified and measured); mutation check: changing one `run.exit` to 99 reports one diff; cost = the corpus layer no longer verifies runtime text; that responsibility moves to locale unit tests / `yx explain` snapshots / generated-artifact reconciliation; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks the mainline: No**                                                                           |
|                                               | 0.5.2 Channel wiring and calibration: wire the pre-commit `cargo test` hook and CI to the `fast` (before local commits) and `full` (before pushes and in CI) dual channels; calibrate the 2600-tier resource profile from measurements (currently extrapolated); add a section on resource markers and the exclusive tier to `test-specification.md` (**awaiting scheduling**; follow-up slice of #486)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | 0.5.1        | The pre-commit `cargo test` hook no longer runs the full suite directly (currently it is `cargo test --all`, the full suite); the “Common Commands” section of AGENTS.md already specifies the fast/full dual channels and the red line against “running the full `--ignored` suite directly”; CI and the pre-commit local path use the same channel semantics; the 2600-tier peak and elapsed time are measured rather than extrapolated; `test-specification.md` has a corresponding details section; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks the mainline: No** |

> **There is no size-baseline task.** The 2026-10-03 decision removed all line-count / size gates;
> size problems are addressed through separation of responsibilities (see `08` Prohibition 2,
> criterion A, which requires human judgment). **0.2.1 is this phase’s core acceptance criterion**:
> if the gate cannot report the known parallel representations, the criterion design is wrong and
> the gate is false.

### P3 Fix Correctness Vulnerabilities (Minimal Approach)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, vulnerability-fix half + **2026-10-07
silent-drop-point audit (3.4)**

> **Routing of semantic corrections (D59, user ruling on 2026-10-11)**: if this track or a later
> phase discovers a defect for which “the old behavior did not count as success”—**A silent failure
> / B silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and uncontrolled resource usage** (crash, stack
> overflow, silently incorrect answer)—it **may be fixed along the refactor**, provided all four
> conditions are met: ① red-then-green (the red criterion must exist first and must actually be
> red); ② do not change the externally visible behavior of programs that “previously compiled
> successfully and ran correctly” (they must not become failures, and their existing diagnostic
> codes/text/exit codes must not be changed); ③ register in the same commit (the row states the
> error class + the phase’s revision note + the required “Error Type” line in the PR block); ④
> regenerate the baseline in the same commit + manually review the diff. **D user-visible semantics
> / E external-contract tables / F RFC-level design changes still require a user ruling first.** For
> the expanded criteria and A–F allowlist, see
> [07 §Routing Criteria for Semantic Corrections](07-equivalence-oracle.md); **“default to a
> super-refactor and register it afterward” is not accepted** (the rationale is in RFC-039 D59:
> without a red criterion, fixes cannot be distinguished from regressions, and this conflicts with
> both “C5′ does not exist” and “no relaxation of any kind”).

      precheck before slot binding and include the union of all candidate parameter names in the
      available column; filter arity-compatible candidates by “name compatibility” in the fallback
      path (sharing `names_support_named_args` with the resolution loop, making the criterion
      single-point), and emit the signature of the uniquely compatible candidate (attributing E1002
      to the candidate containing the name); ir_gen registers mixed names and checks mixed names
      first. Red-then-green: frozen parent commit `10a2dbcb` produced 4 red new tests → `4941f443`
      produced 23 green (acceptance matrices 9/9b/10/11, `named_args.rs`); zero corpus diff (D59

      on 2026-10-25** (commit `cfb3442d`; see the table and [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md) for the
      criterion chain and remaining items); **3.21.2 completed on 2026-10-25** (commit `df89d6ee`;
      all three Class D changes were approved by the user; remaining item in
      [#509](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/509)); **3.21.3 completed on 2026-10-25**
      (commit `a8ad0530`; see the table and [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md) for the criterion chain and
      remaining items); **3.21.4 completed on 2026-10-25** (commit `3b6df553`; a fourth handwritten
      documentation contradiction remains in
      [#509](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/509)); **the entire group was archived on
      2026-10-25**: all four level-3 tasks passed verification/review and were pushed, local gates
      were all green, and each batch’s CI result was checked against coverage relationships (results
      from concurrently running execution surfaces were folded into the unified pre-release check;
      see each issue’s closure comment); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the mainline: No**)

>

- [x] **3.22 Decomposition semantics for function-type annotations** (1 level-3 task, registered on
      2026-10-24, issue [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501); source = F4 traced
      during the round-2 batch review of 4.10; **3.22.1 completed on 2026-10-30** (implementation
      `7880ee17` + fix round `f032582b`—see the table and [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md) for the
      criterion chain, three fix rounds, and two new registrations); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the
      mainline: No**)

- [x] **3.23 Silent degradation when manifest parsing fails / a required field is missing** (1
      level-3 task, registered on 2026-10-25, issue
      [#503](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/503); source = measured during WBS 4.9.8
      implementation—when `yaoxiang.toml` parsing fails (invalid TOML) or a required field such as
      `[package] version` is missing, the CLI **does not report a manifest error** and silently
      degrades to single-file semantics ⇒ the cross-file reference pool fails ⇒ false `W1002`×2
      (`a.yx:1:1` / `b.yx:1:1`, with both types referenced by the entry point), while `check` still
      exits 0; the pre-fix and post-4.9.8-fix behavior is identical item by item (before #465 and
      unrelated to this fix); D59 **Class D** (externally observable behavior), with semantics
      pending a decision (A: report an error and exit / B: continue but emit a W code / C: preserve
      the status quo); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the mainline: No**)

> **Batch (user ruling on 2026-10-14)**: the four small items 3.13 / 3.14 / 3.15 / 3.16 are **highly
> related** (method binding, resolution, and the inference entry point belong to one path; 3.16 is
> another carrier of the same “named argument” criterion—stdlib native exports) and are **merged
> into one team**—the team scope is broadened from “one level-2 task” to “a batch of related small
> items.” The batch composition, rationale, and deviations must be registered when that team closes
> (see the record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)).

the two live in `obligations.rs`, are disconnected, and the method group has no resolution record, so it falls back to short-name matching (the AST contains only `u[0]`/`u==t`, with no `index`/`equal` identifier) ⇒ false positive; Terminal criterion (semantics pending a decision: A report an error and exit / B continue with a W code / C preserve the status quo, finalized after a user ruling): an unreadable manifest **must not degrade silently**—the manifest problem must be reported explicitly (error exit or W code), and the resulting false-positive `W1002` messages must disappear; red-then-green: one red criterion for each of the missing-`version` and invalid-TOML forms (red = zero manifest diagnostics + two false-positive `W1002` messages); (**closure registered on 2026-10-30**: implementation `81d86810` (19 files +415/−77)—`RoleContext` becomes three-state: `RoleContext { surfaces, test_rules, manifest_diagnostics }`: no manifest (NotFound) = the existing legal single-file mode is unchanged; readable manifest = existing assembly; **manifest present but unreadable** (other I/O errors / invalid TOML / missing required fields) ⇒ new code **E3024** (error level; the message contains the manifest path and TOML details; span_exempt per the #324 contract). **Decision A** (2026-10-30, full delegation by the user recorded): treat an invalid `.yx` source the same way—report an error and abort—the diagnostic is injected through the RoleClassification arm (Check/Lsp gated, same as the E3020 precedent; the MultiFile run channel only carries W codes, so no E3024 escapes, with the boundary recorded) and the phase is marked failed, causing only DeadCodeAnalysis, which depends on this phase, to be skipped ⇒ the degraded W1002 messages disappear and the abort occurs before degradation; phases that do not depend on it, such as Typecheck, continue running. The new code follows the four-step E1043 precedent: code table + code-tables --fix + six locales + generator regeneration. passes through Vec, while ProjectConfig parsing still fails and is silently swallowed; the fix takes (a): correct the comment to state the truth, amend it into the commit, and base the verdict **Acceptance**: vulnerability criteria turn green, and **deliberately removing the fix must make
them turn red again**. **Risk**: adding E4018 to the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (changing code first is forbidden);
3.4.3 requires manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 follow C2/C3 and the P9 gate in 9.2. **Risk**: the new
diagnostic in 3.4.1 is a behavior change and requires synchronized corpus-differential baseline and
CHANGELOG updates.

> **Emergency path (ruling on 2026-10-05)**: P3 does not need to wait for all of P2. The only hard
> prerequisites for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red
> criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential testing,
> performance baseline) may proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping the correctness vulnerability
> (`Sorted(3)` passing silently) must not be blocked by snapshot infrastructure. 3.3 (panic to
> diagnostic) has no prerequisite and may land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted on 2026-10-02,
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactor itself; it is a specification-compliance deletion forced by an
ordering constraint.** It is distinct from P10 S5 “reduce the visibility of `pub` items” (Rust’s
`pub(crate)` narrowing).

**Why it must be completed before P4 as a whole**: 029g’s deletion surface overlaps the rewrite
surfaces of four refactor phases. Reversing the order means repeating the same deletions and
baseline updates on the new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| 029g deletion surface                                               | Files                                                                                                              | Rewritten by which phase                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and syntax branches                                         | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexer convergence / 8.3 grammar construction / 8.6 Pratt deletion / 8.7 `parse_assign_after_target` split)                                                                                                                                                      |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                       | `parser/ast.rs:247/270`                                                                                            | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory rename (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                            |
| The `pub` branches of `auto_bind_to_type` and `collect_exports`     | `typecheck/checker.rs:2150/3802/3836`, `environment.rs:284`                                                        | **P5** (the `include!`→true `mod` conversion and checker decomposition are **pure moves**, so the `pub` branches are moved verbatim into the new files)                                                                                                                     |
| Dead-code exemptions `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs:389-407` (inside `check_project`)                         | **P4 4.2.2** (`check_project` slimming)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| The `Public` modifier in `semantic_tokens`                          | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs:450/498`                                                                     | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `ir_gen`’s `is_pub`-ignoring binding                                | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs:1378`                                                                                       | **P7** (SSA conversion of `ir_gen.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| 31 occurrences of `pub` in `src/std/`                               | `std/list.yx`(25) / `json.yx`(4) / `option.yx`(1) / `result.yx`(1)                                                 | No phase changes these directly, but **P6’s hard acceptance requires zero diff in `src/std/` (`git diff --stat src/std/` is empty; direct-driver test files under `tests/` are excepted as described in P6’s hard acceptance section)**—the changes can only land before P6 |

**Additional benefit**: 029g deletes the dead `env.exports` table (populated by the producer and
consumed nowhere in the repository) and the `is_exported`/`is_visible` accessors ⇒ P4’s 4.3
obligation ledger has one fewer “produced but never consumed” field.

| 029g.7 Documentation       | 029g.7.1 Language reference and guides                                                                                       | `reference/language-spec/{modules,syntax,language-overview}.md`, `warning-code/warning-codes.md`, `guide/modules.md` | The specification no longer contains statements contradicting the implementation by claiming that “pub can still be written”; `warning-codes.md:124` (“pub never triggers W1001”) is corrected as well |

**Acceptance (behavior changes, red criteria first)**:

1. **Red-then-green**: land a red criterion before changing code for each of the two behavior
   changes—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② an unreferenced top-level
   binding in a Lib/Script role newly reports W1001 (previously `pub` items were absolutely exempt).
2. **The test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` cases in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and the `is_pub` fixtures, replace them with equivalent new
   cases.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (a new parse error + a new W1001) ⇒
   update the corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG in
   the same commit; zero diff is expected for IR snapshots, and any non-zero diff must be explained.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` are all green.

**Ordering**: overlaps with 3.4 at the file level (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be
serial and cannot run in parallel; **the whole track precedes P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track
does not depend on 3.4; the implementer chooses which to do first based on the context at hand.

> **Commit cross-reference (2026-10-08)**: while this section’s incorporation (the P3.5 chapter +
> main-chain/constraint row) was being incorporated into `97071c81b` during the parallel-flow
> cleanup, that commit’s message records “3.4 addendum registration + D57/D58 ruling documentation”
> and does not mention this track. History is not rewritten; it is registered in place according to
> the established convention (the `d5b18390` precedent, see the P2 landing note), providing a
> retrospective bisect basis.

|                                                  | 4.9.6 Method-reachability criterion (`#450`; registered on 2026-10-13 → **merged into round 2 under the user’s 2026-10-16 ruling A**): without a project root path, a **called method whose overload resolution takes effect** still reports `W1005 Unused method`. Current mechanism (code walkthrough + measurement on 2026-10-16): `compute_reachability` puts only the full name into `reachable`, while `is_reachable` looks up methods in the same set by **short name** ⇒ a method never enters the reachable set (so every method is reported without a pool path); `all_defs` is keyed by full name ⇒ same-named overloads overwrite one another, so only the last one can be reported. Measurement: `method_call_args.yx` / `generic_method_binding.yx` / `interfaces.yx` each produce 3 W1005 messages, while `method_overload.yx` produces only 1 reports W1005 (no relaxation); ④ no conflict with the three round-1 criteria (pool root = package target / `(module,name)` / Script does not use the pool). **Completed in round 2 on 2026-10-17 (commit **Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is migrated with the annotation family in the future, `checker.rs` **must** add
`pub(crate) use annotations::*;` to re-export it, leaving that import unchanged; measurement showed
that 5.3 **did not migrate it**, so this batch added no re-export (the criterion was already
satisfied).

**Only decompose confirmed large blocks.** Do not decompose modules of only 200–400 lines—this adds
navigation cost, reduces cohesion, and provides zero decoupling benefit. The remaining submodules
(signatures / type_defs / imports) are deferred until the files grow back to 1,500 lines.

**Hard acceptance** (**revised on 2026-10-27, following 2026-10-26 decision ②**):
`git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be **empty in every round of
6.1–6.7**. **Direct-driver test files** under `tests/` (tests that directly drive modified code,
such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) may be modified **when mechanically required**, but each
modification must register its **file / line count / reason**; **tests must not be deleted, and
assertions must not be weakened**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavior-fix** batch (new diagnostic
code + baseline update), does not fall under this hard acceptance criterion, and runs as a separate
round with a separate ruling.

> **Revision relationship**: the original criterion (2026-10-26 decision ②) said
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, imposing zero diff across all of `tests/`; the revised
> criterion **changes only direct-driver test files from “zero diff” to “may be changed but must
> register file / line count / reason”**, while the **zero-diff requirements for the corpus layer
> (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and standard library (`src/std/`) remain
> unchanged**. First application = 6.1.2: only `tests/formatter_integration.rs` changed by **3
> lines** (two `assert_format_eq` occurrences changed from `i64`→`Int64` + one comment), with the
> reason = the expected type name in the assertion must be synchronized when the changed spelling
> requires it; no tests were deleted and no assertions were weakened. See 09a “P6 6.1 Name
> Normalization Closure.”

**Risk**: 8.4 dual-parser differential testing is an **unskippable equivalence gate**. If it reveals
non-equivalence, the issue must be resolved before the 8.5 switch, rather than hidden after
switching through C5′. Missing any of the roughly 89 BP-reference migrations in 8.6 will **silently
change associativity** (without an error, only a different parse result).

> **8.3–8.6 depend on D0/D2 from `08`**: placing the grammar file outside `parser/`, or having
> action code refer back to `sema`, will be blocked by `check-boundary.py`.

### P9 Anti-regression Gates (Unified Script Inventory — This Document Supplies the Original Gap G3)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-regression Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative inventory of all CI scripts** (11 scripts). Each script is
integrated into CI at its “introduction phase”; scripts marked report-only are uniformly converted
to hard failures in P9 (from “report” to “fail”). Existing violations (parallel representations,
disambiguation aliases, etc.) cannot reach zero until P6/P8, and converting them to hard failures
early would leave CI permanently red and cause gates to be bypassed manually; when P9 converts them,
the gates should be exactly green. P2’s 2.5, P4’s 4.3.2, and P7’s 7c.1 all reference this table and
do not define separate inventories.

- [ ] **9.1–9.11 Integrate the eleven scripts at their introduction phases and convert them
      uniformly to hard failures in P9** (details are in the table below)
- [x] **9.12 Rebaseline the criterion-C wiring points and clear the stale inventory before
      conversion** (2 level-3 tasks, **registered and closed on 2026-10-26**): the title used at
      registration, “clear the existing inventory of duplicate definitions,” was a
      **mislabel**—violations under `--strict` come only from criterion C (entry-point wiring),
      while criterion A (duplicate definitions, 181 entries) remains report-only and never makes
      `--strict` fail; this was corrected during closure. Root cause: `STAGE_MARKERS` was calibrated
      on 2026-10-06 against the pre-P4 state, where “six paths were each wired manually,” and its
      comment explicitly says “after P4’s unified Driver, this table will be deleted with the
      inventory”; however, after P4 landed, neither the vocabulary nor the 19 `KNOWN_WIRING_SITES`
      entries were retired, so the criterion classified the Driver’s **own phase table**
      (`Stage::DeadCodeAnalysis` variant names, the `slug` string table, the `state.rs` dependency
      table) as violations—the exact opposite of criterion C’s rule text (“adding the Nth entry
      **instead of registering it in the declarative phase table** ⇒ violation”). Disposition =
      **rebaseline the criterion**, not add allowlist entries. Result: `--strict` violations 9→0,
      inventory 19→**7**, measured wiring points 18→**8** (7 inventory + 1 dispatch point),
      criterion-A duplicate definitions 181→180 (deleting the duplicate `build_registry` entry), and
      `pub(crate)` 167→166. **Owning phase: P9; Blocks the mainline: No (blocks conversion of P9 to
      hard failures).**
  - 9.12.1 Rebaselined delivery (four commits, each described in the closure record in
    [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)): ① **criterion rebaselining**—LEX was merged into `Parsing`
    (`src/driver/stage.rs` and 02 §Target Design 1 both state that `Parsing` means “lexing + parsing
    (lex → parse)”), DEADCODE/MONO recognize only call forms, `#[cfg(test)] mod` scopes are exempted
    by **brace pairing**, `dispatch` is listed separately as `DECLARED_DISPATCH_POINTS`, and 13
    stale inventory entries were cleared (each with a removal rationale, classified by measurement:
    function deleted 5 / function still exists but has no phase primitive 5 / only demoted to a
    single phase because LEX was merged 3); ② **arm shapes become derived**—the 12 variants’
    `self.<arm>(` forms are derived from `dispatch`’s exhaustive match (`Stage::ALL` supplies the
    variants, providing a single source) + three fail-loud guards (variant source invalid / arm not
    found / arm has no `fn` definition), and the false report caused by counting the signature line
    as part of the tested body and making `fn check_project(` self-match TC was fixed (`fn_body`
    removes the signature section; measured reduction from “≥2 → <2 phases” was 0); ③ **coverage
    declarations became factual**—the phase implementations of 4 variants (VendorConsistency /
    Discovery / Registry / RoleClassification) are shared primitives outside the Driver, and are
    truthfully registered as known blind spots; ④ **blind spots are described by kind**—a live
    wiring point (`collect_package_refs`, called from `driver/arms.rs:626`) and a dead-code
    candidate (`build_registry`, zero callers) must not receive the same disposition. Three
    independent reviews all returned needs_revision (medium → medium → low; all three findings were
    genuinely fixed), and the fourth round **passed** with no findings. **Acceptance**:
    `python scripts/ci/check-fanout.py --strict` exit 0 (violations 0 / inventory 6 / dispatch
    point 1) ✅
  - 9.12.2 Expand criterion-C coverage and clear `build_registry` (**completed; closed on
    2026-10-26**; user rulings **D1-A retain + D2-A delete**): ① **delete** the zero-caller chain
    `build_registry` / `build_registry_from` (formerly `orchestrator.rs:379-396`) and `discover`,
    which thereby lost its only caller (formerly `:416-418`); `DiscoveredFile` / `Discovery` /
    `extract_module_info` / `parse_file` are all retained; because `src/lib.rs:27 pub mod frontend`
    → `frontend/module/mod.rs:20 pub mod orchestrator` makes `build_registry` a **public lib-target
    item**, deletion was separately ruled on as an external API change. ② **Retain** the
    Driver-external phase primitives that still exist after deletion: VendorConsistency ←
    `ensure_vendor_consistency`, Discovery ← `discover_with_used`, RoleClassification ←
    `roles::classify` (narrowed to the qualified form—a bare name would falsely match same-named
    helpers in other modules, as demonstrated by a constructed case), and Registry ←
    `extract_module_info` (does not use bare `.register(`, whose name occurs in multiple places in
    the repository). ③ Exactly one newly exposed site: `collect_package_refs`
    (`driver/helpers.rs:1057`, live code, called from `driver/arms.rs:626`) was registered,
    increasing the inventory 6→7 and measured wiring points 7→8. ④ The criterion’s docstring
    coverage declaration was changed to reflect facts: the old “6+6” division was merged into
    **10+2**, R1–R3 determinate retention criteria were added, the statement “any function
    constructing this phase’s data is not retained” was deleted because it was disproved by five
    table entries, and three stale line numbers / unmeasured figures and two inaccurate comments
    were fixed (`orchestrator.rs:7` module header, `statements.rs:737` referring to a deleted
    function). Behavior-neutral: 356 corpus rows byte-for-byte identical, 216 snapshots zero drift,
    `cargo test --lib` 2855/0/2, `pub(crate)` 167→166 (an inevitable result of deleting `discover`).
    Review chain: round 1 `needs_revision` (1 medium + 2 low) → fix → round 2 **pass**
    (independently reproduced with a custom probe); followed by the F4/F5/F6/F8 cleanup round →
    narrow review **pass** (AST statement-by-statement comparison confirmed that the criterion had
    no collateral changes). **Owning phase: P9; Blocks the mainline: No.**

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Chain

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 emergency path (ruling on 2026-10-05)**: P3’s prerequisites are narrowed to **2.3.2 + 2.4.1 +
> 2.4.3** (the multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR verifier, 2.2
> snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential testing and performance baseline) may proceed in parallel with
> P3. Stopping correctness vulnerabilities must not be blocked by criterion infrastructure; however,
> **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified Driver requires all three layers of criteria to
> be in place.

| Constraint                | Reason                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P0 → P1                   | The three prohibitions constrain every action in P1 (1.2.x adds `mod` declarations to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                                                                               |
| P1 → P2                   | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| P2 (after narrowing) → P3 | Vulnerability criteria must be **written as red first** (2.4.1/2.4.3), and the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist for the red tests to run in                                                                                                         |
| P3 → P4                   | Fix bugs before refactoring; reversing the order would fossilize bugs as “established behavior” in the phase table                                                                                                                                            |
| P3.5 → P4                 | RFC-029g (#399)’s deletion surface overlaps the rewrite objects of P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8 (see the inventory in §P3.5 of this document)—reversing the order means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, new checker layout, and new IR (D56) |
| P4 → P5                   | The `include!` conversion and checker decomposition modify the same file                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| P5 → P6                   | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated so that regressions can be located by bisection                                                                                                                                                        |
| P6 → P7 / P8              | If type representations do not converge first, the new IR will grow into a third representation                                                                                                                                                               |
| P7/P8 → P9                | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 and the `pub(crate)` leak count in 9.3 take their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                                                    |

### Parallelization Groups

| Group                                      | Members                                                                            | Basis                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only one authorized by the RFC)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | Disjoint file sets: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                               |
| B                                          | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Independently add `mod` to each file; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| C                                          | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three layers of criteria are independent                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| D                                          | 4.4 (proof-layer wrap-up) ∥ the entire P6 line                                     | `02` explicitly states that S5 “should proceed independently of S1–S4”                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| E                                          | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | All are independent after 4.1                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| F                                          | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced in P0 may be developed in parallel) | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| G                                          | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | Disjoint file sets (verified on 2026-10-05: S3 modifies `docs/src/dev/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 modifies `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 modifies two `pub` sites under `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; no pairwise overlap) |

**Cannot run in parallel (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (B6/E5 in
the same batch); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (`checker.rs` / `environment.rs` are
the same files—the external track’s `pub` deletion batch and the silent-channel fixes must be
serial).

**New internal documentation constraint**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly serial;
7a→7b→7c→7d is strictly serial, and the `Phi` arm in 7b must precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is an independent phase rather than part of P9**—gates are “post-hoc checks,” while rules are
  “pre-hoc judgment.” P9’s scripts cannot prevent “patching when a refactor is required”; only the
  D0–D4 review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative inventory of CI scripts**—the three coexisting inventories caused
  by original gap G3 made closure impossible. This table assigns each of the 11 scripts to one phase
  and makes the report-only status and conversion point explicit.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an “independent track to do after the
  refactor”**—its deletion surface overlaps the rewrite surfaces of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039 D56).
  Deferring it until after the refactor means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, new
  checker layout, and new IR; this is the same lesson as incorporating `2315`/`2341` into 7e and RFC
  status corrections into S4.
- **P5’s steps are supplied by this document rather than merged into P4**—P4 is already the largest
  single-point risk, and adding checker decomposition would exceed independently revertible
  granularity.
- **5.3.2 uses “zero caller changes” as hard acceptance**—the decomposition must be independently
  revertible.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says “the fixed defects
  should not be rolled back” and lacks a requirement to record “what was fixed.” **Execution
  convention**: every commit fixing an 1.3.x defect must cite the fixed test name and defect
  description in its commit message, providing a retrospective bisect basis.
- ~~**7b.1 is too coarse**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: split into 7b.1a / 7b.1b dedicated commits
  under DoD 6.
- **8.2.1 is too coarse**—about 89 reference renames; missing one will **silently change
  associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding level-3 tasks**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: D17 landed as 7e.1,
  and D20 landed as 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34 opcode reclamation was incorporated into 7e.1, while the
  deletion action remains in P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally assigned to an “independent issue”**~~
  **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core
  language semantics and must not be left for a future implementation.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()`) has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**:
  incorporated into P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode-gate extractor) has ambiguous ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated
  into the generation-time gate task in P10 S6.

## Conflict Registry: All Resolved

> **The original “Conflict Registry” C1–C8 and all open questions in the individual documents have
> converged into the [RFC-039 Decision Registry](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md)
> (D1–D50).** This document retains no pending items. The mapping is:

| Original number | Issue                                                                          | Decision                                                                                                                                                 |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| C1              | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8                      | **Revive** (P1’s prerequisite is that tests with real assertions are revived), and P8’s 8.6 deletes the file together with Pratt                         |
| C2              | The “grammar-driven” commitment vs table-driven parsing                        | **Fully implemented**: use LALRPOP, as stated in `05` “Syntax: Fully Grammar-Driven”                                                                     |
| C3              | Conflict between the C6 category definition and S1 classification              | **Classify S1 under C6** (revival requires no equivalence criterion, only regression tests); expand the C6 definition to “pure deletion or pure revival” |
| C4              | Allowlist for `test_release_plan_spans_consumed`                               | **No allowlist**; the difference must be empty; if it cannot be empty, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective → change it to `PlanId` (D20/D41)         |
| C5              | Internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions remain unverified | **P4’s 4.2.7 lists this as a mandatory verification item**, to be confirmed line by line during implementation                                           |
| C6              | The wasm playground fix requires P4’s `ProgramKind`                            | **Merge the wasm-path vulnerability fix into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                                                      |
| C7              | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                             | **Assign to `04`** (P7 batch c), see D21                                                                                                                 |
| C8              | Fixing `upvalue_count` and the `.42` version number                            | **Fix + bump `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`), see D17                           |
